Then at dinner I’ll try, especially if my wife cooked, but I end up just picking at it. It’s only been a few weeks, so maybe it’s not a big deal, but the loose jeans made me think I should mention it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve really noticed that, no. I mean, I still like the things I usually like, I think. Maybe I’ve been a little less on top of doing stuff, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest exactly. I don’t know if that counts as “down,” but… yeah, I guess I have been.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a noticeable amount of weight, beyond just your jeans feeling looser?\n\nPatient: I haven’t actually weighed myself, so I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more that I noticed my jeans are looser, and that kind of lined up with the last few weeks of not eating much—skipping breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, then just sort of picking at dinner. What made me bring it up is more that for the past few weeks my eating’s been off—like I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without meaning to, then just kind of pick at dinner even when she cooked—and my jeans are starting to feel loose. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been waking up with this heavy, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been about three weeks now. What made me bring it up is more that for the past few weeks my eating’s been off—like I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without meaning to, then just kind of pick at dinner even when she cooked—and my jeans are starting to feel loose. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the heavy, flat feeling you wake up with has anything to do with your lack of interest in eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they’re probably connected. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Does the heavy, flat feeling you wake up with usually stay with you throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, most days it kind of does. I mean, it’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, but that heavy, flat feeling is there when I wake up, and it doesn’t really shake off much. Even if I try to pick up my guitar or do something normal, it doesn’t lift me the way it usually would. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been waking up with this heavy, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been about three weeks now. It kind of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule, though I don’t want to overstate it or anything. But yeah, around three weeks sounds right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any difficulty with sleep?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, I am sleeping, so it’s not like I’m up all night or anything… but it’s been kind of broken up. I’ll wake up around four or five and start thinking about bills, or tracks I haven’t finished, and then I don’t really feel rested. I’m already tired before the day starts, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like… making plans or wanting to do something to myself. I guess I’ve had moments where I feel really worn down, but I wouldn’t call it suicidal. I’m okay saying that, right? Like, I don’t want to worry anyone unnecessarily.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven’t weighed myself or anything. It’s just been the last few weeks I’ve been forgetting breakfast and lunch, then barely eating dinner, and my jeans feel a little loose. So… maybe a few pounds? I haven’t weighed myself or anything. It’s just been the last few weeks I’ve been forgetting breakfast and lunch, then barely eating dinner, and my jeans feel a little loose. So… maybe a few pounds? I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Are you just not interested in eating when you skip meals, or is it that you're not thinking about food at those times?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more that I’m not thinking about food, mostly. Like, I don’t wake up deciding not to eat or anything, I just kind of… get to later in the day and realize I skipped breakfast and lunch. Then at dinner I’ll try, especially if my wife cooked, but I end up just picking at it. It’s only been a few weeks, so maybe it’s not a big deal, but the loose jeans made me think I should mention it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve really noticed that, no. I mean, I still like the things I usually like, I think. Maybe I’ve been a little less on top of doing stuff, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest exactly. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, thanks for seeing me. I guess what finally made me make the appointment is… my eating’s been kind of off the last few weeks. It’s probably not a huge thing, but I’ll realize I skipped breakfast and lunch without meaning to, and then at dinner I’m just sort of picking at it, even when my wife made something.\n\nAnd, uh, my jeans are feeling a little looser, so I thought maybe I should mention it. Does that seem like something worth coming in for?\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No, not intentionally. I mean, I wouldn’t mind being a little healthier, I guess, but I haven’t been trying to lose weight. It’s more like I just don’t think to eat, and then by dinner I’m only picking at food. The jeans feeling looser is what made me think maybe I should say something.\n\nDoctor: How much weight do you think you've lost?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I haven’t weighed myself or anything. It’s just been the last few weeks I’ve been forgetting breakfast and lunch, then barely eating dinner, and my jeans feel a little loose. So… maybe a few pounds? I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Are you just not interested in eating when you skip meals, or is it that you're not thinking about food at those times?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more that I’m not thinking about food, mostly. Like, I don’t wake up deciding not to eat or anything, I just kind of… get to later in the day and realize I skipped breakfast and lunch. Then at dinner I’ll try, especially if my wife cooked, but I end up just picking at it. It’s only been a few weeks, so maybe it’s not a big deal, but the loose jeans made me think I should mention it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve really noticed that, no. I mean, I still like the things I usually like, I think. Maybe I’ve been a little less on top of doing stuff, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest exactly. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not sad exactly all day, maybe, but most days lately I wake up with this kind of heavy, flat feeling. Like the apartment feels too quiet, and even if I pick up my guitar, it doesn’t really lift me the way it usually might. I don’t know if that counts as “down,” but… yeah, I guess I have been.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a noticeable amount of weight, beyond just your jeans feeling looser?\n\nPatient: I haven’t actually weighed myself, so I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more that I noticed my jeans are looser, and that kind of lined up with the last few weeks of not eating much—skipping breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, then just sort of picking at dinner. So… noticeable maybe, but I don’t know an exact amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to buy new clothes or alter any of your existing clothes due to weight loss?\n\nPatient: No, no—I haven’t had to buy anything new or alter clothes. It’s not like that, at least not yet. I just noticed my jeans are fitting looser, and that’s what made me mention it. The past few weeks I’ve been skipping breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, and then just picking at dinner, even when my wife cooks. So… maybe it’s more appetite than a big clothing-size thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the weight loss has been significant enough that others have noticed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think anyone’s really said anything, no. My wife maybe noticed I’m not eating much, but I don’t know that she’s noticed weight loss exactly. What made me bring it up is more that for the past few weeks my eating’s been off—like I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without meaning to, then just kind of pick at dinner even when she cooked—and my jeans are starting to feel loose. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the heavy, flat feeling you wake up with has anything to do with your lack of interest in eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they’re probably connected. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but most days lately I wake up feeling kind of heavy and flat, like the apartment is just too quiet. And even things I usually like, like picking up my guitar, don’t really lift it. So eating just feels… not that appealing, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Does the heavy, flat feeling you wake up with usually stay with you throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, most days it kind of does. I mean, it’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, but that heavy, flat feeling is there when I wake up, and it doesn’t really shake off much. Even if I try to pick up my guitar or do something normal, it doesn’t lift me the way it usually would. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been waking up with this heavy, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been about three weeks now. It kind of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule, though I don’t want to overstate it or anything. But yeah, around three weeks sounds right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any difficulty with sleep?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, I am sleeping, so it’s not like I’m up all night or anything… but it’s been kind of broken up. I’ll wake up around four or five and start thinking about bills, or tracks I haven’t finished, and then I don’t really feel rested. I’m already tired before the day starts, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like… making plans or wanting to do something to myself. I guess I’ve had moments where I feel really worn down, but I wouldn’t call it suicidal. I’m okay saying that, right? Like, I don’t want to worry anyone unnecessarily.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the heavy, flat feeling you've been experiencing is mild, or is it interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s mild, mostly. I mean, I’m still doing the basic stuff around the house, so it’s not like everything has fallen apart. But… it is slowing me down. My projects have kind of stalled, and I’ve missed a few chances to follow up with musicians I’d reached out to. So it’s interfering some, I guess, just not in a dramatic way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the heavy, flat feeling is worse in the morning and improves a bit as the day goes on?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s mostly right. Mornings are usually the worst. I wake up and there’s this heavy, kind of flat feeling, like the apartment is too quiet, and even picking up my guitar doesn’t really do much for me at first. I’ll wake up around four or five and start thinking about bills, or tracks I haven’t finished, and then I don’t really feel rested. I’m already tired before the day starts, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like… making plans or wanting to do something to myself. I guess I’ve had moments where I feel really worn down, but I wouldn’t call it suicidal. I’m okay saying that, right? Like, I don’t want to worry anyone unnecessarily.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the heavy, flat feeling you've been experiencing is mild, or is it interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s mild, mostly. I mean, I’m still doing the basic stuff around the house, so it’s not like everything has fallen apart. But… it is slowing me down. My projects have kind of stalled, and I’ve missed a few chances to follow up with musicians I’d reached out to. So it’s interfering some, I guess, just not in a dramatic way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the heavy, flat feeling is worse in the morning and improves a bit as the day goes on?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s mostly right. Mornings are usually the worst. I wake up and there’s this heavy, kind of flat feeling, like the apartment is too quiet, and even picking up my guitar doesn’t really do much for me at first. It can ease up a little later, maybe once I’m moving around, but most days lately it’s there when I wake up.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling less interested in doing things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um… probably about three weeks, I think. It kind of started after my wife got settled into her full-time work schedule. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but yeah, that’s around when I noticed I wasn’t really getting pulled toward the things I usually enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “no longer,” exactly. It’s more like… they don’t grab me the same way. Music, guitar, reaching out to people for projects—those are still things I care about, I think. They just feel kind of harder to get into lately. But it’s not like I hate them or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s more than 5%, exactly… I haven’t really weighed myself. But what made me mention it is my eating’s been kind of off the past few weeks. I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, and then even at dinner I kind of just pick at it, even when my wife cooked. My jeans are starting to feel a little loose, so… maybe? I’m not trying to make a big thing of it, but it does seem different.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your heavy, flat feeling as being most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think most days, lately.
